# KNOWN DEVIATIONS

Deliberate, audited departures from the C++ original (snort3 @ fe29cbe7, 2026-11-05).
Each entry cites the original source so parity stays measurable and auditable.

## API shape (mechanical, no behavior change)

- `helpers/bitop.rs` (bitop.h): `set(unsigned int)` takes `usize`; original never
  type-checked larger widths either. Buffer ops identical.
- `helpers/base64_encoder.rs` (base64_encoder.cc): C `encode(data,len,buf)` pointer
  pair becomes `&[u8]` + caller-provided `&mut [u8]`. `nullptr,0` call in the
  original's "null data" SECTION is just `&[]` here. Step machine is line-for-line.
- `helpers/ring.rs` (ring.h/ring_logic.h): the C++ atomics in RingLogic exist only
  because snort used them across threads with relaxed discipline; the port is
  single-threaded `&mut` (Rust enforces it). Index arithmetic (rx=0, wx=1,
  usable=size-2, `full()` never true because it tests count()==sz) preserved
  verbatim including the C++ quirk.
- `helpers/ring.rs::Ring::new` requires `T: Clone + Default` in place of
  C++ `new T[size]` default-construction.
- `helpers/utf.rs` (utf.cc): C `decode_utf(..., int* bytes_copied)` out-param
  becomes `(ok, copied)` tuple return. `convert_character_encoding` (iconv bridge)
  NOT ported yet — depends on libc iconv; returns no equivalent in this layer.
  CharsetCode/CharsetSrc discriminants kept numerically identical (HTTP
  inspectors compare these values).
- `helpers/boyer_moore.rs` (boyer_moore.cc): C `toupper()` (C locale, uint8_t)
  reimplemented as ASCII-only mapping — same as the original's behavior since the
  original relies on locale "C" in unit tests. `search` returns `i64` with -1 for
  miss, mirroring C `int`. Skipping tables index bytes 0-255 exactly.

## Not yet ported (see PORTING_PLAN.md for status)

Everything outside `helpers/` — codecs/, detection/, framework/, main/, etc.
`helpers/{hyper_search, literal_search, json_stream, markup, streambuf, ring2,
lockless_ring, grouped_list, ber, ...}` are next on the queue.

## sfip (sf_ip.rs / sf_cidr.rs / sf_ip_test.rs)

- `inet_pton`/`inet_ntop`(glibc path) reimplemented in pure Rust rather than
  FFI to libc, for hermetic unit tests. Behavior matched to glibc on Linux
  (strict v4 leading-zero rejection is additionally enforced by SfIp::pton,
  exactly like the original). The SfIp::test_features ntop mode is selected
  by an explicit `test_features: bool` arg instead of a static bool.
- `SfIp::set_str()` mirrors `set(src, nullptr)` and therefore FAILS on
  CIDR/masked inputs (C++ returns SFIP_INET_PARSE_ERR when !srcBits and
  bits != 128). Pass-through variant `set_addr_str()` returns (ret, bits)
  mirroring `set(src, &outbits)`.
- `set_check` in tests mirrors `memcmp(&ip1,&ip2,sizeof(struct))` by
  comparing ip8+family (C padded-struct compare includes no other live state).
- ntop returns String; C returned a caller-owned char buffer.
- `SfIpRet` added `LookupUnavailable(17)` (present in current sf_returns.h)
  beyond the C test-harness codes[] array (17 entries) — same numeric layout.

## helpers/grouped_list.rs GroupedList<T>

- C++ raw self-referential linked list ported to an index arena owned by the
  container. Observable semantics (list1 iteration order, group chains with
  push-on-front `group = this`, GrpRef pointer-to-pointer head updates,
  leave_group repeatedly-callable no-op, erase counts) verified against all
  16 Catch sections ported 1:1 to tests/grouped_list_test.rs.
- delete_element() tombstones (value taken via Default) rather than freeing;
  group refs walking a deleted element would be UB in C++ — here they read a
  clean None. No shipped test path hits that case.
- Holder value requires T: Default (C++ default-constructs it).

## ports + parser/parse_ports
- PortObject::group (RuleGroup*) omitted: the detection layer that owns it is
  not ported yet; the field is inert for all ports-layer operations.
- PortVarTable is a HashMap rather than snort GHash (LRU/primetable internals
  are out of scope at this layer). find() returns an owned clone; the only
  C++ caller immediately PortObjectDup's the borrowed pointer, so behavior is
  identical. dump_rule_info add-if-missing gated behind an explicit flag.
- PortObjectParseString returns Result instead of null + errflag in POParser;
  errflag/message readable via POParser::err()/error_string() equivalents.
- POParserGetShort strtoul emulation: 64-bit unsigned long saturation then
  C (int) wrap, matching the observed C++ exactly (100:4294967396 -> 100:100;
  21+ digit runs -> POPERR_BOUNDS). Verified against harness vectors.
- ParseWarning console output (duplicate ports) not surfaced; same 0 return.
- snort3 has NO Catch2 unit suite for ports/parser (unlike sfip/helpers), so
  parity is enforced by replaying ground-truth vectors generated from the
  ORIGINAL C++ sources built out-of-tree (tools/cpp_harness; copy tree is
  gitignored and regenerated by build.sh).

## sfip/sf_ipvar + sf_vartable
- C linked lists (`sfip_node_t*` chains) ported as `Vec<SfIpNode>` with
  cursors; the separate `head_count`/`neg_head_count` counters and their
  C mutation quirks (single-node `list_contains_node` overlap path not
  decrementing count; tail-append loops not decrementing lengths) are
  preserved, so `merge_len` over-counts exactly where the C original does.
- `sfvt_add_str` returns `(SfIpRet, Option<index>)` instead of an out-param
  `sfip_var_t**`; the table owns the vars and `lookup_var` borrows. C++
  callers that mutate the returned pointer in place (and expect the table
  entry to reflect it) map to mutating a clone in tests; observable table
  state after each *add/define* matches C++.
- C stores var `name` as `snort_strdup`'d char* that may be NULL; port uses
  `Option<String>` and treats names as always UTF-8-unsafe-free like C.
- `sfvt_print` (debug dump) and the per-thread `sfip_var_t` arena cleanup of
  `sfvar_alloc`'s `snort_api` pool are omitted; no test consumes them.
- Alias vars (`sfvar_create_alias`) deep-copy lists but share `id` and
  `value`, mirroring C's shallow alias fields.

## helpers/literal_search + boyer_moore_search
- HyperSearch backend omitted (hyperscan is a C library dependency); the
  instantiate() path always returns the Boyer-Moore engine, which is what
  C returns when HAVE_HYPERSCAN is off. Matcher behavior is identical for
  the literal use case covered by the tests.
- C BoyerMooreSearch borrows the caller's pattern pointer; the Rust port
  stores a copy (same bytes) so the object cannot outlive borrowed data.
- C search() bumps a raw pointer by skip[]; Rust slices. skip[c] <=
  pattern_len <= buffer_len in every reachable state, so bounds match.

## helpers/lockless_ring
- Direct translation: same free-running u32 head/tail counters, same
  EMPTY/STORING/STORED/LOADING slot protocol, same round_up_to_power_of_2.
- T: Default replaces C value-initialization of slot payloads.
- USE_MOVE_TO_DEQUEUE template flag kept but inert: Rust reads the payload
  by pointer-move in both branches (C's copy branch needs T copy-ctor).
- C size() else-branch quirk preserved verbatim (head + (UINT32_MAX -
  tail) is 1 below the true wrapping difference past 32-bit rollover).

## helpers/ring2
- Direct translation of the cursor/guard/turn protocol; store is a fixed
  Vec<u8> in UnsafeCell with raw-pointer access (no &/&mut ever alias the
  shared store between the SPSC threads). Validated under Miri with
  -Zmiri-strict-provenance: no UB, no data races.
- Reader::read returns an owned Vec<u8> instead of C's borrowed pointer
  into the store; byte-for-byte the same record, observable difference
  only if a caller dereferenced the record after pop() (no test does).
- Writer::write_len_check(len) exposes C's early length validation
  (data_len == 0 / > UINT32_MAX checked before touching data) so the
  "Oversized record" Catch section is testable without a 4 GB slice.
- C test's per-test-case Ring2 objects are independent test fixtures here;
  Rust re-creates the buffer per #[test] (Catch SECTIONs shared one
  TEST_CASE object; each SECTION started from a fresh REQUIRE empty state,
  so this is equivalent).

## helpers/json_stream
- Sink is dyn std::io::Write instead of std::ostream&; byte output identical
  for all test content.
- C std::quoted(key) emulated: '"' and '\\' backslash-escaped, everything
  else verbatim (glibc default quote/escape chars).
- isprint uses the C "C" locale set (0x20..0x7E) explicitly.
- put() overload set split by name (put_null / put_i64 / uput_u64 /
  put_cstr / put_string_q / put_string / put_f64 / put_true / put_false)
  because Rust has no overload resolution; put_cstr(None) models C's
  put(key, nullptr) -> "null", empty c-string/empty std::string short
  circuits preserved.
- C++ iostream fixed+precision is sticky stream state; here each
  put_f64 formats the value with its own precision ({:.*} == printf %.*f,
  same rounding), so no stream-state leakage either way.

## helpers/ber + framework/cursor core
- Cursor ported with only the fields ber uses (name/buf/size/pos/delta/
  extensible/buf_id/file_pos). Packet* wiring, the CursorData side-table,
  extensible-buffer growth APIs, and the copy-ctor semantics remain TODO
  with the detection layer.
- C Cursor borrows an external buffer pointer (tests point it at
  sub-array views, e.g. buf+4); the Rust Cursor owns a Vec and sub-array
  views are materialized (set("test", &buf[4..])) so ber's cap check
  `total > UINT32_MAX - (c - beg)` computes identically: beg is the view's
  offset-0 base, exactly like C's beg = buf + 4.
- BerReader holds beg/cursor/end as offsets (usize) instead of raw
  pointers; deref guards (cursor+1 > end etc.) kept verbatim.
- C BerElement::data is const uint8_t*; here the offset into the Cursor
  buffer. Both tests comparing data == buf + N check the same number.

## log/obfuscator
- C std::set<Block, by-offset> -> BTreeMap<u32 offset, usize length>:
  identical offset-ordered iteration and identical offset-only element
  identity (the C comparator treats blocks with equal offsets as equal,
  which std::set emplace dedupes on); push()'s replace-if-longer kept.
- C's two-iterator cursor (cur_buf map iterator + it set iterator)
  becomes cur_buf: Option<String> + it: position index; first()/next()
  re-walk the sorted vec. C's UB-ish paths (deref cur_buf when the map
  is empty, next() before first()) return false here instead; no test
  depends on C's behavior there (EmptyList2Test requires next()==false).
- mask_char exposed as const; C header's per-instance getter is static
  in practice (constexpr member).

## utils/util_unfold
- sf_unfold_header/sf_strip_CRLF/sf_strip_LWS take &[u8]/&mut [u8] +
  Option<&mut i32> out-params; C's NULL-buffer guards become length
  checks and are unreachable (callers can't pass null slices).
- sf_unfold_header NUL-termination quirk preserved verbatim: writes NUL
  at out only when n < outbuf_size, else at outbuf_size-1 (which, given
  the loop bound n < outbuf_size, can leave a non-NUL-terminated buffer
  of exactly outbuf_size bytes plus an overwritten last byte, exactly as
  original).
- output_bytes (C *output_bytes = outbuf_ptr - outbuf) returned as the
  second tuple element; matches upstream CHECK(output_len == input_size)
  and CHECK(output_len == output_size) semantics.

## helpers/streambuf
- streambuf get/put-area pointers (eback/gptr/egptr, pbase/pptr/epptr)
  modeled as i64 offsets into the owned store; C pubsetbuf(char*,n) takes
  &[u8] lifetime for input; for output, the `new char[n]` the tests pass
  is taken as Vec<u8> (Rust cannot express caller-owned new[]; the
  take_data() owner-transfer contract is preserved as Vec handoff).
- libstdc++ public entry points (in_avail/sgetc/sbumpc/snextc/sungetc/
  sputbackc/sgetc-pbackfail/sgetn/sputc/sputn/pub*) replicated from
  /usr/include/c++/14 semantics; tests use an istream/ostream emulation
  with the sentry/gcount/badbit/tellg/tellp mechanics of libstdc++ 14
  (read sets eofbit|failbit on shortfall; write -> badbit on sputn
  shortfall; tellg/tellp return -1 when fail/bad; sentry blocks on bad).
- ostreambuf_infl::xsgetn reads [pptr,epptr) like the original quirk;
  C leaves those bytes uninitialized, Rust guarantees 0 (Vec zeroing).
  No upstream test depends on the garbage values.
- C setbuf(nullptr,n>0) on the output buffer is UB-adjacent (setp to a
  null base); Rust collapses it to an empty buffer. Unexercised upstream.

## utils/util
- check_file_size(const char*) instead of FILE* (fileno indirection);
  rotate_file_for_max_size deferred (pure errno/rename/umask plumbing,
  no upstream tests) pending a filesystem-error strategy shared with
  other file-facing subsystems.
- ts_print takes an explicit TimeCfg {thiszone, output_use_utc,
  output_include_year} instead of reaching SnortConfig::get_conf();
  time formatting uses civil_from_days (Hinnant) standing in for
  gmtime_r; verified against date -u.
- str_to_int_vector: C++ std::stoul throws std::out_of_range/
  invalid_argument on bad items; Rust panics on those inputs (same
  crash-level behavior); stoul's 64-bit ul saturation mapped to u64
  saturating + mod 2^32 (C unsigned long was 64-bit there, stoul
  returns unsigned long; callers store into uint32_t so the C
  narrowing was mod 2^32 — pinned in tests? not yet: noted).
- get_random_seed: reads 4 bytes of /dev/urandom with clock fallback
  (C std::random_device behavior varies by libstdc++ config).

## framework/value (+ parameter subset, utils/bits)
- get_addr over-size case: C std::string::copy past the end reads
  uninitialized bytes; Rust zeroes (tests only pin the all-zero result).
- get_addr_raw exact-size case leaves trailing bytes zero in Rust where
  C left string past-the-end bytes; C callers memset first, tests match.
- Value is Clone (std::string members make C copyable too; C has no
  user-defined copy of stringstream* leak — ss is C-leak-on-copy, Rust
  clones it, stricter and safe).
- strtoul: glibc octal overflow clamps at LONG_MAX while decimal wraps
  at ULONG_MAX (probe-pinned); C strtoul(-x) wraps (probe: -1 ->
  ULONG_MAX). Rust replicates both exactly.
- Value::strtol of magnitude == 2^63: glibc strtol saturates LONG_MAX
  (probe), not wraps — modeled.
- str_to_int_vector in utils/util: C std::stoul throws on out-of-range
  (64-bit ul); Rust returns mod-2^32 like the implicit uint32_t
  narrowing the C callers applied before the exception path mattered
  (deviation noted there; revisit when the option parser pins which
  callers exist).
- Parameter: range union (Parameter*/RangeQuery*/char*) and validate()
  deferred to the option-parsing layer; index/find_ported verbatim.
- FixedBitSet<N> = std::bitset<N> subset for the option bit lists.

## hash (hash_key_operations, ghash, primetable)
- HashKeyOperations: only the static_hash constants (seed 3193/scale 719/
  hardener 133824503) are implemented; the randomized ctor needs a
  bit-exact std::mt19937 + uniform_int_distribution port — deferred until
  the detection layer selects per-thread salting (flagged for revisit).
- GHash: typed payload D + arena index handles instead of void*/GHashNode*;
  userkey/userfree map to Rust ownership/Drop. Keys always stored owned
  (C copied unless userkey), including the string NUL terminator, so
  do_hash sees strlen+1 bytes exactly as C.
- GHash(0,...): C constructs then SIGFPEs on first % nrows; Rust
  constructs and panics on first use. nrows<0 => -nrows literal (no prime
  lookup): preserved.
- find_first/find_next: C find_first() advances the cursor before
  returning the head (verified from source; mid-port "fix" reverted).
  C find_next() after remove()-of-current returns a dangling pointer
  (the upstream drain loops rely on freed chunk ->next staying readable:
  UB); Rust ends the scan cleanly at the freed slot — upstream drain
  loops terminate in Rust via the same protocol because the cursor was
  already advanced before the removal.
- dup insert leaves the ORIGINAL data untouched (HASH_INTABLE short
  circuit): pinned by GH find-b2 (7, not 8).

## framework/ips_option + pdu_section
- IpsOption is a concrete base struct here (name+type+hash+==); C's
  virtual eval/action/retry/get_pattern live in the detection layer.
  `option_eq` stands in for operator== (Rust has no user ==, and PartialEq
  is reserved for structural equality).
- Instantiator statics (set_priority/classtype/sid/...) deferred: they
  mutate IpsInfo/RuleStateMap which don't exist yet.
- PduSection::PsError value: C enum sequence => 5 (PS_MAX aliases
  PS_TRAILER=4); section_to_str has 5 entries (no ERROR string).

## hash cache layer (memcap_allocator, hash_lru_cache, xhash, zhash)
- HashNode* raw pointers -> arena indices (Option<usize>) into XHash.nodes;
  link/cursor update order kept verbatim (incl. HashLruCache touch/remove
  cursor rewiring to gprev BEFORE unlink).
- C `void* data` -> `usize` payload handle chosen by the caller (test suites
  use indices into their own payload Vec; NULL_PAYLOAD sentinel for
  `insert(key, nullptr)`). With datasize>0 C memcpy's into an inline blob;
  the port never copies payloads, only handles.
- memcap accounting quirk preserved: free_() decrements unconditionally even
  for foreign nodes; Rust saturates mem_allocated at 0 instead of unsigned
  wraparound (C double-free accounting is UB; pinned in memcap tests).
- xhash test free_anr_lru_delete_free_node_test relies on delete_lru_node()
  returning *false* right after release_node (node sits on the free list, not
  in the LRU cache) — C CHECK(ret == HASH_OK) only passes because false==0.
- XHash::full() quirk preserved: true iff the FREE LIST is empty.
- allocate_node order pinned: free list -> memcap alloc (max_nodes gate) ->
  ANR release_lru_node; ANR walk bounded at XHASH_MAX_RECOVERY_WALK=64 in
  current source (2026 commit), pinned by release_lru_node_walk_is_bounded.
- ZHash uses a Box<dyn HashOps> stand-in for the virtual do_hash; zhash_test
  stubs FlowHashKeyOps::do_hash as unfinalized seed*scale+byte accumulation,
  ported verbatim as flow_test_ops (the real flow_key.cc bob-jenkins mix
  variant defers to the flow layer).
- MemCapAllocator::allocate() returns bool (not the calloc'd pointer); the
  arena allocates the node slot only when accounting permits, matching the
  observable allocation-count behavior.

## hash/lru_cache_local
- C++ template <Key,Value,Hash> -> Rust <K,V> (HashMap; C Hash param had no
  observable behavior in the suite). entry_size passed in explicitly instead
  of compile-time sizeof math; tests pin 2*sizeof(Key)+sizeof(Value)+8
  (x86-64 std::list iterator).
- C returns `Value&` from find_else_create; Rust returns a stable arena handle
  (entry()/set_entry). C references stay valid across splice by luck of
  std::list; the handle model has identical observable reads/writes.
- Constructor WarningMessage on clamp is not emitted (log layer not ported).
- External stats struct by reference -> owned stats + get_stats() copy.

## hash/lru_cache_shared + lru_segmented_cache_shared
- Data = std::shared_ptr<Value> -> Rc<RefCell<Value>>: C tests mutate the
  pointee through the shared_ptr; RefCell models that dynamically and the
  single-threaded port needs no mutex (public lock()/unlock() omitted; C
  cross-thread pinning semantics are provided structurally by Rc liveness).
- Purgatory retained as Vec<Data> (pruned payloads outlive the operation,
  same as C++ delaying destructors past the lock).
- C quirk pinned: stats.bytes_in_use/items_in_use are NEVER updated by
  LruCacheShared itself; suite asserts both == 0. stats struct field order
  is load-bearing (C get_counts() reinterprets it as the peg array).
- LruCacheLocal prunes while current>max AND list.size()>1; LruCacheShared
  prunes while current>max AND !list.empty() (can evict every entry) —
  asymmetry between the two classes preserved.
- SegmentedLruCache get_segment_idx XOR-folds raw key bytes (C
  reinterpret_cast<const uint8_t*>(&val)); Rust reads via
  std::slice::from_raw_parts on the aligned Copy key. segment_count must be
  a power of two (asserted as in C).
- SegmentedLruCache::set_max_size uses integer division per segment, e.g.
  C++ with size 17 over 4 segments => 4 each. C get_max_size() sums the
  per-segment caps (may be < requested); preserved.

## framework/range (RangeCheck)
- C `RangeCheck` methods take `const char*`; Rust `parse`/`validate` take
  `&[u8]` (the rule layer feeds ASCII). `validate(s, r)` takes
  `Option<&[u8]>` for the nullable `r` bound string.
- C `get_num` uses `strtoll` + errno; Rust `c_strtoll` models ERANGE by
  saturating at LLONG_MIN/MAX and *failing* the parse on overflow, matching
  errno != 0 -> false probes: "+9223372036854775808" fails,
  "-9223372036854775809" fails, "-9223372036854775808" succeeds at i64::MIN.
- Octal quirk pinned: "08" fails because strtoll stops at '8' and get_num
  requires the whole token consumed; "0123" = 83 octal.
- `hash()` narrows i64 min/max to u32 exactly as C casts int64_t members to
  uint32_t before mixing; op is hashed as its C enum discriminant (0..8).
- The C probe harness (tools/range_harness) exercises private members because
  the class exposes min/max as protected; the Rust port publishes them
  (range.cc's own UNIT_TEST block does the same via friend access).

## utils/sflsq (SF_LIST)
- C `NODE_DATA = void*`; Rust `SfList<T>` requires `T: Copy` (pointer-shaped
  payloads). Arena indices stand in for `SF_LNODE*`; `ListCursor` stands for
  the `SF_LNODE**` out-parameter.
- `sflist_free`/`free_all`/`static_free_all` reduce to ownership drop in
  Rust; `free_all(cb)` keeps the callback-per-node observable behavior.
- Probe replay pins gcc's right-to-left printf argument evaluation: the
  `rmh/rmt ... cnt=N` lines report the count read *before* the removal call.
- C `sflist_first(nullptr)` sets *v = null and returns null (the `if (!s)`
  guard); Rust cannot name a null list so that branch is unreachable.

## parser/parse_utils
- C `ParseError(...)` writes to the message subsystem; Rust returns error
  strings in the result struct so probes/tests can assert position text
  ("invalid byte code at N", format/range messages) verbatim.
- C `errno` is process-global; Rust `parse_int` returns `errno: i32` with
  the Linux ERANGE value (34). The value returned on the invalid-format path
  is the *unclamped* strtol value, as in C (early return before clamping).
- C state-0 char dispatch relies on `isdigit`/`isspace`/`isxdigit` called on
  possibly-negative chars; for >127 bytes glibc returns 0 for all and the
  Rust ASCII predicates agree, so behavior is identical.
- `parse_byte_code` takes `&[u8]` (C `const char*`); the closing-quote check
  `in[len-1] == '"'` with the decrement-then-stop loop is replayed exactly,
  including the interior-quote entry into hex mode.

## parser/arg_list (ArgList)
- C stores `char*` into argv + a `std::string buf` backing single-char keys;
  Rust clones owned `Vec<u8>` tokens. The `buf.assign(s,1)` quirk (short-opt
  key is exactly one char) is preserved on purpose, not cleaned up.
- C's truthiness test on the pending `arg` pointer distinguishes `--`
  (pending = empty string, still non-null -> flushes key="") from no pending
  arg; Rust models this with pending = Some("") which matches, since the C
  pointer for `--` is to the NUL terminator and thus non-null.
- `get_arg` cannot yield the C sentinel "" vs null ambiguity for val; empty
  Vec means C "".

## framework/ips_action + main/policy (IpsPolicy action variables)
- C keeps the action registry in file-statics mutated at plugin load
  (PluginManager -> ActionManager::get_interface); Rust threads an explicit
  &mut ActionRegistry so tests control registration order deterministically.
- IpsAction* payloads: policy.action / configured_action hold Option<()>
  placeholders until the actions/ layer exists; get_action()'s selector-vs-
  type-array indexing split (selector 0 -> action[type], else
  configured_action[selector]) is ported verbatim around the placeholders.
- C policy.cc cannot compile hermetically (DAQ, DataBus, Shell); its 3 Catch
  cases were ported by reading and pinned indirectly via the compiled-
  original ActionManager registry harness (tools/action_harness).
- get_interface duplicate-name quirk pinned: unordered_map::emplace does not
  overwrite, but s_act_index++ fires unconditionally, so a re-registered name
  burns a type index forever (vector MAX2 = 7 with only 6 names).
- get_action_priorities alert_before_pass expansion appends "alert pass "
  with trailing space and skips the normal separator logic (`continue`),
  reproduced exactly including double-space artifacts on adjacent entries.

## parser/vars + parser/var_dependency + parser/parse_conf (ParseIpVar)
- C logs ParseError/ParseWarning/ParseAbort through file-globals with
  printf semantics, and ParseAbort longjmps out of every frame. The port
  pushes ordered ParseMsg values onto VarContext.diag and returns
  ParseAbort as Err (message recorded in diag first). No abort unwinding
  is attempted beyond the Err: C's longjmp targets (recovery points,
  DAQ, Shell) do not exist in the port yet.
- C reaches the three var tables + var_id through get_ips_policy() and
  the two weak lists through var_dependency.cc file-statics. The port
  threads one &mut VarContext; resolve()/set_variable() take a split
  borrow (&mut list + &mut ctx) — the C callback *can* push to the same
  weak list mid-resolve (is_resolving_* guards the push path instead);
  the split borrow makes that structurally impossible, which is exactly
  what C's guard enforces for every reachable path.
- VarEntry: C circular doubly-linked list hung off IpsPolicy; the port
  keeps a Vec with head at index 0 and reproduces insert-after-head
  (t.insert(1)) so walks match the C head->next chain. addrset
  (sfip_var_t*) is not carried at this layer — nothing in vars.cc sets
  it; the config_file layer will attach it with DeleteVars support.
- DeleteVars: C frees the circular list (incl. addrset); the port drops
  the Vec (Rust drop) — same observable end state: dp->var_table null,
  var_id NOT reset (C keeps incrementing; ports match).
- VarSearch: C constructs std::string(ipvar->value) even when value is
  nullptr (UB; empty in practice with libstdc++). Port maps None -> "".
- parse_conf.cc ParseIpVar cannot compile hermetically (detection/rules.h
  -> OptTreeNode, DAQ...). The harness compiles the verbatim function
  body in stubs.cc; the vectors exercise the REAL branch behavior
  (DUPLICATE warn, LOOKUP_FAILURE push vs resolving-error, DEFAULT
  cross-table PERR).
- WeakVariable::is_visited / is_resolved are per-list-entry C flags; the
  port replays them exactly (visited set before recursion, resolved set
  before the callback) — pinned by the RESOLVE/RESOLVE2 stuck-state
  vectors where a longjmp left is_resolved=true burned in the list.

## utils/cfmt (C-printf engine) + log/messages.{h,cc} + log/log_errors.h
- snort3 formats through glibc vsnprintf/printf/syslog. The port has zero
  external crates, so utils/cfmt re-implements the C-printf subset the log
  layer actually uses, pinned byte-for-byte against glibc by the
  tools/log_harness vector battery (P17/P17b: ~30 format strings). Not a
  general printf: no positional args ($n), no %a/%td beyond slots, no
  wchar, nonlocalizable.
- Arg values are passed as a typed slice (I/U/F/S) instead of C varargs;
  the int-slot masking (bare %d keeps low 32 bits sign-extended, %hd
  %16-bit wraps etc.) reproduces the ABI truncation the C call sites get.
- FatalError: C _exit(EXIT_FAILURE)s; the port returns Fatal::Quitting
  and keeps already_fatal on the engine so the reentry guard matches
  (second call prints NOTHING, returns Reentrant = C _exit(1)).
  in_main_thread() worker-abort branch and cleanup_fatal_error() stay with
  the embedding binary.
- log_safec_error: C assert(false) (SIGABRT in release-less builds); the
  port panics after the <1000-gated ErrorMessage. The harness vector for
  P14 includes the glibc assertion note line (prints the harness's own
  path) — stripped from the replay expectation; everything before it is
  compared exactly.
- parse_errors/parse_warnings/reload_errors and the reload description are
  plain u32/String, not std::atomic (snort3's are process-wide atomics;
  the port's engine is single-threaded per-policy). get_parse_errors/
  warnings stay destructive read-and-clear like C; get_reload_errors does
  NOT reset.
- warning_flags / logging_flags / output_flags / log_quiet_bypassed are
  SnortConfig CLASS statics in C: they persist even when get_conf() ==
  nullptr. The port keeps them directly on LogEngine (faithful: no deref
  needed to read a static) plus a conf_present bool standing for
  get_conf() != nullptr, gating exactly the two sites that deref the
  object (ParseWarning's suppression guard, WriteLogMessage's buffered
  check). The get_quiet/syslog/buffered accessors mirror the C inline functions.
- BatchedLogger (use_log_buffered / OUTPUT_FLAG_LOG_BUFFERED) is not
  ported: WriteLogMessage panics if the bit is armed, matching the
  harness abort() that made the path observable.
- ConfigLogger: C's 12 log_limit / 9 log_value integer overloads collapse
  to signed/unsigned pairs (i64/u64); disable-vs-unlim precedence and
  the (long long) casts keep output identical. log_list keeps the C
  quirks: head fmt "%*s%s%.0s%s" swallows the prefix (printed only on
  continuation lines), null caption prints glibc "(null)", the 75-char
  wrap test is res.length()+val.length() BEFORE adding ' '+val, and the
  uncaptioned log_list(list) variant uses its own no-leading-space
  accumulation + tab\t continuation.

## parser/parse_conf.rs + parser/config_file.rs (config core)
- C++ file-statics (`files` location stack, `rules_file_depth`,
  `s_ips_policy`, `lua_conf`, `snort_conf_dir`, and parser.cc's
  `s_special_includer`) are modeled as explicit state on ParseConf /
  ConfFiles / SnortConfig. The Rust engine must thread them because
  there are no process-wide mutable globals here.
- `assert(arg)` in get_config_file/parse_include: the port relies on
  call sites never passing null (the probe's nullptr parse_rules_file
  case is modeled by Option, not by a failed assert).
- ParseAbort semantics: ExpandVars inside parse_include longjmps in C,
  skipping everything after the logged abort. The port returns early
  with the abort already in the diag sink, which produces the same
  observable trace (abort text logged, no PERR follows, no push).
- parse_stream (rule layer) is out of scope; a StreamSeam callback +
  stream_reads counter stands on the seam. The harness's stubbed
  parse_stream re-enters parse_include to pin rules_file_depth and the
  includer vs get_parse_file vs special_includer seed selection; the
  test drives the identical reentries through the same seam.
- std::ifstream open test is approximated by stat success: nonexistent
  -> err, directory "opens" (vector: adir no PERR), which matches C on
  Linux for the paths exercised. EACCES-only files (ifstream fails,
  metadata succeeds) are indistinguishable in the current call sites;
  revisit when file-policy paths land. stat uses metadata() (follows
  symlinks, like stat(2)); C valid_file uses stat(2) as well.
- get_error(errno): C reads errno after the failed ifstream open
  (ENOENT in practice); the port hardcodes strerror(2) at the single
  call site, linking glibc strerror directly (extern block), keeping
  the vector text byte-exact.
- NetworkPolicy in main/policy.rs carries only the checksum subset
  (eval/drop + the four inline accessors + checksum_drops); CodecManager,
  DataBus, InspectionPolicy vectors, FilePolicy defer with detection.
- The C Location `const char* code` null-vs-empty distinction is kept
  via Option<String>: print_parse_file renders "?" only for null
  (vector #C5 vs #C6).
- push_parse_location's C++ default argument (line = 1 in the
  declaration, 0 in the parse_conf.h shim used by the harness) is an
  explicit parameter in the port; call sites pass 1 like the real
  header default.

## log/text_log.rs + main/thread.rs (buffered logging + file naming)
- C TextLog is a malloc'd header + flexible char array behind a FILE*.
  The port uses a Vec buffer sized maxBuf+1 (the extra slot is the C
  buf[maxBuf] NUL slot) and a std::fs::File; glibc line buffering
  (setvbuf _IOLBF) is irrelevant because every byte reaches the handle
  through a TextLog_Flush write_all — the flush points are the file-
  stream boundary.
- fatal_error mid-roll (TextLog_Roll's reopen drops is_critical to the
  true default, vector H4) cannot _exit in a library: the port marks the
  TextLog dead, and every later operation is a silent no-op, mirroring
  that the C process was gone. The parity test clears already_fatal per
  "forked child" exactly as the probe's fork boundaries did.
- C stdout inside TextLog is the literal FILE*; the port injects a
  StdoutSink so the replay can order fwrite output against marker
  printfs the same way the merged probe stream did.
- TextLog_Size for the stdout handle is 0 in the port; in C it is
  fstat(1)->st_size but is never observable (Roll bails on stdout first
  and maxFile comparisons on an unrolled stdout stream always flush).
- thread.cc's populate/invalidate_instance_maps (CSV writer) defer with
  the thread layer: ThreadConfig::get_instance_tid etc. are stubs in the
  harness; the naming half is fully ported with the umask/mkdir(0770)
  and id[8] 7-digit snprintf truncation quirks intact.
- The C probe stubbed time() (constant fake clock, explicit bumps). The
  port threads a Clock callback; production callers wire
  SystemTime::unix_epoch. TextLog_Roll calls now() three times like C
  (suppression check, name, last=) so a real clock's tick mid-roll
  stays as C-observable as it was there.

## parser/parse_so_rule.rs (so-rule stub FSM)
- C walks const char* as raw chars; the Rust port scans bytes and pushes
  chars from byte values. Rules reaching --dump-dynamic-rules are ASCII
  (the FSM only compares against ASCII punctuation and std::isspace is
  the C-locale set), so byte-scanning is equivalent; a non-ASCII UTF-8
  byte would behave exactly as the char value C's (signed) char would for
  isspace purposes — none of the comparison branches treat it specially.
- C's `if (*accum == stub)` guard compares CONTENTS (std::string
  operator==), and both operands alias when accum==&stub. The port keeps
  a Target enum (Stub/Opt) and replicates: Stub side always sets prev,
  Opt side only when opt content == stub content (vectors exercise both
  the equal and unequal Opt paths).

## rule-tree core (detection/{rules,sfrim,treenodes,signature} + parse_rule + parser RTN lifecycle)
- `RuleTreeCache` (parser.cc XHash) keeps C's insert/release shape but looks
  `get_user_data` up by RTN-pointer identity instead of bucket-chain
  `key_compare`. C's `RuleTreeHashKeyOps::do_hash` re-reads RTN content, so
  every key_compare match provably lands in the query's own chain — but the
  chain ORDER for multi-match buckets (own entry vs never-released orphans
  from deleteRtnFromOtn remove=false) is not pinned by any locked vector.
  The transcript pins the observable outcome: reduce_rtns never re-shares a
  head through find_rtn ("shared p1=0 p2=0") and every node keeps its own
  RTN. If a future vectors block pins bucket order, revisit here first.
- `OtnMap` is an insertion-order Vec, not a GHash(10000): every observation
  site in scope (OtnLookup point lookups, otn_count/dup counters,
  reduce_rtns/find_first traversal feeding finish_ports counters) is
  order-insensitive in the locked transcript — summed counters and point
  lookups only. GHash traversal order would only matter for a future
  dump_rules/ShowPolicyStats block.
- PortTable merged-port compile path (PortTableCompile/Finalize/GHASH) is
  not part of this layer; the rule core consumes only the INPUT-list half
  (FindInputPortObjectPorts/AddObject) with identity-preserving handles,
  which is what the transcript exercises (spo/dpo names, noname sharing,
  finish_ports counters incl. bleedover-limit gate and any-any conversion).
- FastPatternConfig ctor: harness copy replaces the ctor body (real one
  resolves MpseManager::get_search_api and drags the MPSE monolith);
  search_api stays null and is never read on any locked path. Same shape
  mirrors into the Rust port (fp_config.rs knobs).
- SoManager::get_so_eval is an abort-witness in the harness; s_so_rule is
  never set (probe has so-stub OTNs, not so-rule parse state), so the Rust
  so-channel models the gid/sid/rev copy + addRtnToOtn-so branch with
  so_rtn unused, exactly as witness semantics pin it.
- ParseAbort == FatalError: C terminates the process; the Rust port returns
  Err(Fatal::Quitting) after emitting the identical diagnostics. The probe
  pinned both fatal port channels as FATAL_DEMO subprocess blocks; the
  replay test drives the same calls in-process, asserts Fatal::Quitting
  (the rc=1 counterpart), and greps the diagnostics through the exact
  build.sh filter (`grep -e FATAL -e Quitting`).
- FatalError rc: probe exits 1 via the main-thread cleanup+exit path
  (set_thread_type(STHREAD_TYPE_MAIN)); cleanup_fatal_error internals
  (event/alert flush) are the embedding binary's and remain deferred with
  the log-output layers.
- `parse_rule_state` (rule-state stub): C frees rtn->sip/dip + header and
  deletes the otn; Rust models this with ownership drop. The RuleState add
  itself (key from policy_id + sigInfo, s_rule_action snapshot) is literal.

## parser/parse_stream (row 17b)

- C file-static counters (`chars/tokens/lines/comments/keys/rules/lists/strings`) live in a
  `StreamCtx` the driver owns; `rules` is parked on `RuleCoreState.stats_rules`. C prints none
  of them in the locked scope (`printf` lines are commented out upstream), so their home is
  plumbing, never observable transcript.
- C `parse_stream` reaches `inc_parse_position`/`parse_include`/`get_config_file` through
  globals; the port threads `&ParseConf` (+ `SnortConfig`/`ConfFiles` args) instead. The C++
  `ifstream`/`stringstream` is modeled by a byte cursor + `std::fs::read`; the ENOENT branch
  pins glibc `strerror` text via the existing parse_conf port.
- `parse_include`/`parse_rules_file` re-entry is fused into `parse_stream.rs` (single channel)
  rather than reusing `ParseConf::parse_include`'s seam closure: the seam type cannot hand the
  recursion the live `RuleCoreState`, and C has no such boundary. `ParseConf::parse_include`
  remains for conf-layer tests (row 16q).
- `exec(FSM_END)` calls `parse_rule_opt_end` only when `rps.otn` is Some. C derefs
  `rps.otn` unconditionally, but that statement is reachable on a null otn only when
  `parse_rule_open` returned null mid-body (s_ignore set), and upstream `parse_rule_opt_end`
  early-returns on exactly `s_ignore` — observable behavior identical (skip is silent).
- `State.punct` is `Option<&str>`: `None` models C `nullptr` (the `if (s->punct)` guard skips
  the table update), `Some("")` models C's non-null empty table (update CLEARS). One edge NOT
  pinned by vectors: while `punct == nullptr` (SP/DIR/DIP/SPX-region rows carry null punct)
  C's `strchr(punct, c)` is UB; glibc resolves the NUL and MATCHES c == '\0'. The Rust helper
  returns false for `None`. No locked vector feeds NUL through that state, so the difference is
  unobservable in the transcript; flagged here as the one intentional simplification.
- TT_NONE-valued fsm rows (the two num-14 SET/ADD rows) carry `ttype 0` in Rust exactly like
  C, where the get_state test is `!s->type || type == s->type`; TT_NONE tokens never reach
  get_state (the loop breaks) — kept verbatim.


## detection/rtn_checks.{h,cc} (rule-header check arms; row 17c)

- `Packet` is NOT ported in this layer (its ctor drags DAQ + flow + stream
  internals). `rtn_checks.rs` evaluates against `PacketLite`, mirroring EXACTLY the
  four things the checks read: `p->proto_bits`, `p->ptrs.sp`, `p->ptrs.dp`,
  `p->ptrs.ip_api.get_src()/get_dst()`. `IpApiLite` keeps ip.h's get_src/get_dst
  `type != IAT_NONE` gates and the real `IpApi::set(const SfIp&, const SfIp&)`
  semantics (type=IAT_DATA, plain copies). `has_port`/`sfvar_ip_in` are the
  already-pinned ports/sfip ports, so the arm bodies are literal transcriptions.
- C's hand-linked `RuleFpList` chain is the `Vec<RuleFpList>` installed by
  `setup_rtn_func_list` (row 17); each `fp_list->next->RuleHeadFunc(...)` becomes
  `call_head(i + 1)`. `eval_rule` mirrors the production entry fp_detect.cc:355
  (`rtn->rule_func->RuleHeadFunc(p, rtn, rtn->rule_func, check_ports)`).
- `CheckBidirectional` quirk kept EXACT: the function ignores its `RuleFpList*`
  param and ends `return 1;` (rtn_checks.cc:149), so on a bidirectional rule the
  `Proto`/`End` nodes linked AFTER it never run. Pin: RC922 icmp (tcp rule, icmp
  packet, nets match) -> `cp1=1`; a running Proto node would have returned 0.
- `CheckProto` returns the RAW `proto_bits[spid] & p->proto_bits` value, not 1
  (C returns the AND expression): pinned 2/4/32/64 by eval + ARM lines.
- `check_addr_port` models C's `if (!rule_addr) goto bail;` + null-safe
  `sfvar_ip_in` (`if (!var || !ip) return false;`) with Options: a null
  `pkt_addr` (IAT_NONE ip_api) fails the IP test rather than crashing. Not
  reached by locked vectors (the probe always sets real ips via IpApi::set);
  kept honest for future Packet integration.
- Harness boundary for real packet.h (both logged at build.sh): `#include
  <daq_common.h>` dropped (DAQ not linked; witness POD header supplies
  `DAQ_PktHdr_t`/`DAQ_Msg_h`/the two macros — none read by locked paths, and the
  mock Packet never sets `pkth`), `#include "flow/flow.h"` swapped for the
  flow_fwd witness exposing only the members packet.h's inlines touch
  (`Flow::flags.client_initiated`, `ssn_state.snort_protocol_id`, the
  FilteringState POD). Nothing in the harness derefs flow (mock flow=null).
- `copy/protocols/ip.cc` compiles VERBATIM; the only link witness is
  `layer::get_inner_ip6_frag()` (ip.cc's off/id accessors; mock packets never
  reach those paths).
- `s_ignore` cross-stream LEAK is upstream behavior, now pinned by vectors:
  latched by `parse_rule_type` mid-stream (S2), never cleared because EOF means
  that rule's `parse_rule_close` never runs (the sole clear site is 1162-1164 —
  the 1573 site is `#ifdef UNIT_TEST`-only), and it SWALLOWS the first rule made
  in-process afterwards. Production snort shares the same file static across
  `parse_rules_string` calls, so this is behavior to reproduce, not fix.

## framework/codec.{h,cc} (row 17d)

- `IpProtocol`/`ProtocolId` are NOT Rust enums. C declares enumerators with
  the SAME value (`IP == HOPOPTS == ETHERTYPE_NOT_SET == 0`;
  `PROTO_NOT_SET == RESERVED == PORT_SCAN == 255`) and `IPV6ExtensionOrder`
  leans on that aliasing when it casts `(ProtocolId)ip_proto`. A Rust enum
  would forbid the duplicates and silently change which arm matches, so the
  subset here is `pub type` + consts modules
  (`ip_protocol::HOPOPTS == ip_protocol::IP == 0`, etc.) preserving both the
  aliases and the cast. Full protocol tables ship with the codecs row.
- The `Codec` CLASS is half-deferred: its virtual `decode/encode/update/
  format` vtable and the `CodecApi` plugin struct are part of plugin loading,
  out of scope for this build. What IS ported is everything framework/codec.cc
  holds: `codec_event`, `CheckIPV6HopOptions`, `CheckIPv6ExtensionOrder`
  (as free functions; the C probe reaches them through a ProbeCodec subclass
  that `using`-declares the two protected members, so the translation is
  honest), `Buffer`, `EncState`, `CodecData`, `CompoundLayer`, `RawData`, and
  the header constant tables.
- `CodecData` drops the `conf` back-pointer in practice (kept as an Option
  field but never dereferenced by the pinned vectors) and `EncState` drops
  the `const ip::IpApi&` reference (the IpApi lands with the packet layer;
  nothing in `get_ttl`/`*_set()` touches it). `RawData` drops `daq_msg`
  (DAQ is row 20). `Codec::PKT_MAX` and `CDAPI_VERSION` are ported verbatim
  alongside them.
- Harness witness seams (all logged at build.sh): `detection/detection_engine.h`
  witness whose `queue_event` prints `EV gid sid` (the real header drags the
  whole detection engine); `catch/snort_catch.h` replaced by a plain-catch
  shim (the snort `TestCaseInstaller` needs the snort main binary; the four
  `[buffer]` TEST_CASEs register identically, and the gate compiles the
  VERBATIM upstream `#ifdef UNIT_TEST` block from codec.cc); `utils/util.cc`
  + `log/messages.cc` not linked (real ones drag snort_config), their needed
  symbols (`snort_strdup/strndup`) are verbatim-body witnesses in stubs.cc;
  `layer::get_inner_ip6_frag` link witness (layer.cc itself needs packet.h).
- Quirks pinned verbatim by the 66-line transcript: hop-by-hop `switch`
  `default:` falls THROUGH into the TLV length check (a non-enum type latches
  `CODEC_IP6_BAD_OPT` + EV 279 and STILL runs the TLV block — H3/H4/H4b
  separate the latch from the 295 length error); the `raw.len <
  total_octets` TRUNCATED event does NOT return (H6: EV 272, then the walk
  continues to EV 295, `return true` from `ip6_opt_receiver_skips`);
  `ip6_opt_receiver_skips` uses the RFC 8200 0xC0 action bits; the
  second-"Destination Options" exception needs routing-seen AND the NEXT
  id's order == `IPV6_ORDER_MAX` (O4/DSTOPTS-next fires UNORDERED on calls
  2-3; O11/IPV6-next stays silent); `CODEC_LAYERS_EXCEEDED` is spelled `0x400`
  upstream — identical to `CODEC_IP6_EXT_OOO` — kept as written, not "fixed";
  `Buffer` base starts one byte PAST the array (the C comment says so; probes
  pin it via array-relative `data_off`); `allocate()` leaves public `off`
  untouched while `clear()` zeroes it; `get_ttl` reverse path computes
  `MAX_TTL - lyr_ttl` then clamps to `MIN_TTL`.
- C printf evaluation order nuance: the FIRST lock of these vectors relied
  on GCC emitting the side-effecting `CheckIPV6HopOptions(...)` call (via the
  event witness writes) BEFORE the label `printf`'s other args read it — but
  that's not guaranteed by the standard. The probe was rewritten to bind the
  return value in a statement first (evaluation-order-safe), and the
  relocked transcript (md5 28c79a7f88c8...) is the locked truth.

## time/periodic.{h,cc} (row 17e)

- `std::list` at file scope is mirrored by a `thread_local!` `Vec` (same
  order, same splice-at-index insert, `retain` for `remove_if`). Snort's
  periodic machinery is single-threader; no pinned vector observes
  cross-thread sharing, so per-thread storage is fidelity-neutral here.
- C compares hook function pointers with `==` (pointer identity). Rust does
  the same via `std::ptr::fn_addr_eq`, which is the defined form of the C
  comparison (bare `fn` `!=` is linted for codegen-unit instability).
- No witness seams needed: the only upstream include is
  `main/snort_types.h` (SO_PUBLIC), and the upstream test guard is the
  `CATCH_TEST_BUILD` macro (guarded `TEST_CASE("periodic", "[periodic]")`
  block compiled verbatim in-harness); probe + Catch gate both use the real
  translator with zero stubs.
- Quirks pinned: equal-priority registration is LIFO (the insert scan stops
  at the first node with priority >= the new one, and insertion happens
  BEFORE it); `check()` reloads `time_left = period` on fire, so period p
  fires every p+1 checks (period 0 = every check); the arg pointer is
  dereferenced at CALL time (label changes between register and fire
  change what fires); `unregister_handler` is by hook fn pointer and removes
  ALL nodes with that hook (P5: one call removes both duplicates).

## utils/stats/response_buf.{h,cc} (row 17f)

- The two externals beyond the TU (`ControlConn` and the global
  `snort::LogMessage`) become traits: `ControlConn::respond(chunk)` (C
  calls exactly one site, `respond("%s", data)`, so the chunk arrives
  pre-formatted; the witness reproduces the `%s` NUL truncation) and
  `LogSink::log_message(chunk)`. The full ControlConn class belongs to the
  control/shell layer; only this seam is needed here.
- The C++ test/probe drives the buffer through `std::ostream`, whose
  behavior lives in libstdc++, not in this TU. `EmuOstream` reproduces
  `__ostream_insert`/`__ostream_write` and `ostream::write` with semantics
  read from /usr/include/c++/14 (sentry skips once bad; sputn shortfall
  sets badbit -- note `operator<<(const char*)` writes
  `traits::length(s)` bytes), and `xsputn` is the verbatim
  `bits/streambuf.tcc` loop. badbit is the STREAM's flag, so it lives on
  the driver, not on the buffer.
- C++ vtable dispatch of the protected `overflow`/`sync` through the base
  streambuf becomes direct methods; `flush`'s buffer is snapshotted before
  the sink call (borrow split; identical bytes). The destructor flush is
  the explicit `dtor()` at the C destruction point -- the upstream suite
  observes it AFTER its CHECKs, and the replay preserves that order.
- Gate has ZERO stubs: the upstream `response_buf_test.cc` ships its own
  `ControlConn` (c-tor/respond) and `snort::LogMessage` implementations,
  which link against the verbatim `response_buf.o`.
- Quirks pinned in vectors (md5 591a73538b0f...): 1023-byte effective
  capacity (setp end = sizeof-1); flush writes `*pptr = 0` and send_chunk
  treats the buffer as a C string, so a chunk with an embedded NUL logs
  ONLY the prefix (W9) and an all-NUL chunk is silently skipped without
  touching either sink while the pending area still resets (W10); a
  failed flush leaves the put area intact so the destructor flush re-sends
  the SAME bytes (W7's pair of identical 1023-x lines -- and the upstream
  suite itself never CHECKs this pair, the probe does); overflow(eof)
  returns not_eof after a no-pending sync, and EOF_INT only when sync
  fails.

## utils/stats snapshot+renderer + framework/counts.h (row 17g)

- `Value` is `std::variant<monostate,bool,i64,u64,double,string>` ->
  Rust enum, one variant per alternative; `std::get_if` becomes a match.
  `Counter` keeps C's borrowed/owned name split: `set_name(const char*)`
  borrows (Rust spells the C lifetime contract `&'static str`, which is
  what every pinned call site passes), `set_name(const
  std::string&)` owns; `get_name()` prefers borrowed exactly as C.
- `std::deque` nesting -> `Vec` (append-order only is observable);
  `std::optional` -> `Option`.
- The renderer's externals are trait seams, NOT placeholders:
  `StatsLogSink` stands at `set_log_conn/LogLabel/LogText/LogCount/
  LogStat` (log_stats.cc's THREAD_LOCAL `s_ctrlcon` routing and
  `LogStat`'s own formatting belong to the log layer's later row --
  `LogLabel/LogCount/LogText` already ported there for row 17 stay
  separate from this seam), and `MemoryReport` stands at
  `memory::MemoryCap::print(verbose, initial)`. `SnortConfig::
  log_verbose()` (a static) becomes `RenderEnv::log_verbose`.
- C++ iomanipulators are STREAM STATE: `setw` applies to the next insert
  only; `right/left` stick; `append_table_line` saves/restores
  `line.flags()`. The Rust port emits per-insert `{:>25}`/`{:<10}`/etc.
  format strings -- same bytes, no surviving state (deviation is the
  mechanism, not the output; the 44-line transcript is byte-exact).
  `1e6` formats as `1000000.000` (C++ `ostringstream << fixed << setprecision(3)`),
  which Rust `{:.3}` reproduces.
- Harness: `main/policy.h` and `main/snort_config.h` are the VERBATIM
  proven rules_harness grafts (data_bus_wit, PolicyMap private->public,
  api_options `#define API_OPTIONS 0`); this TU reads only `log_verbose
  ()`, grafted with the real upstream body + `LOGGING_FLAG__VERBOSE =
  0x00000001` (snort_config.h:112). The upstream suite compiles verbatim
  as-is: it `#include`s `../output_text.cc` directly, so the gate tests
  the FILE-STATICS at function granularity, and ships its own log/memory
  witnesses (zero stubs).
- Quirks pinned (md5 523e29b3f031...): first-column truncation is a
  BYTE resize at 25 (`x-that-is-over-twenty-fiv`); counter names
  truncate the same way before LogCount (so the logged name itself is
  cut); `counter.percent` is NEVER consulted by the text renderer (only
  `total` decides LogStat vs LogCount -- S3 pct-only counter); tables
  render before counters in a group; a group with no renderable content
  is skipped without emitting its label while the section label above
  still prints (S7); section-name equality with "Summary Statistics" is
  exact (trailing space misses the MemoryCap side effect, S6);
  `render_text` calls `set_log_conn` twice (conn then nullptr) -- and
  the probe passes nullptr at entry, so 2 nulls / 1 call pin.

## utils/stats/output_json + helpers/json_stream touch (row 17h)

- The existing `helpers/json_stream.rs` port gained
  `put_string_bytes(key, &[u8])`: C `std::string` is a byte container,
  and pinned probe inputs (0x01-0x03, 0x80) are not valid UTF-8, so a
  `&str`-only API cannot feed them. The escape layer was already
  byte-based; this is the same body, byte entry.
- `put_value`'s trailing C `else json.put(key)` arm covers `std::
  variant` exhaustiveness; the Rust match over the `Value` enum is
  exhaustive without it (same observable behavior).
- Harness: three upstream suites link into ONE gate binary (11 + 6 +
  basic). The embedded `-DUNIT_TEST` CU of json_stream.cc must REPLACE
  the plain object (same TU) or the link duplicates every symbol --
  same pitfall as codec_harness, now with a build.sh comment.
- Pinned quirks (documented, not fixed): JSON renderer writes the
  `percent` key the TEXT renderer ignores; Value::Str goes through
  put_string (empty renders `""`) while table COLUMNS go through the
  early-returning overload (empty column = zero output, no separator);
  null char* emits `null` (split consumed) but empty char* emits
  NOTHING (split not consumed); empty std::string key-put likewise
  consumes no separator; `close()` endls only at level 0 with
  level_array 0; the tables array opens for a group with ONLY
  row-less-but-present tables... when the group is otherwise
  renderable, so `"tables": [  ]` appears (J3/J5 pair); escape uses
  C-locale isprint on raw bytes -- 0x7f is NOT printable-escaped
  (`\u007f`), matching the `\u00xx` lowercase form.

## utils/util_jsnorm.{h,cc} (row 17i)

Structural (safety-relevant, behavior preserved):

- C threads ONE shared byte buffer through the nested decodes by
  pointer aliasing (SFCC output buffer = Unescape src = Unescape dst;
  Unescape output = PNorm src = PNorm dst). Rust passes a per-stage
  snapshot read view + the same write buffer. Safe because every scan
  step writes at most one byte where it has already consumed one, so
  the write cursor never overtakes the read cursor of the stage.
- Dbuf `data` pointer + size/len -> `size`/`len` over a caller-owned
  slice. All C pointer arithmetic (`overwrite`, `cur_ptr`,
  outBounds) becomes plain index arithmetic.
- `iis_unicode_map` (raw `uint8_t*`, caller-owned, possibly null) ->
  `Option<&[u8]>` cloned into the decode state. C's `unicode_map[iNorm]`
  with iNorm up to 0xffff would read far past any real table (UB); the
  Rust port substitutes NON_ASCII_CHAR for out-of-table indexes —
  unreachable with the pinned 256-byte map in the transcript.
- `strtoul(buf, &p, radix)` -> hand-rolled same-radix accumulation
  (digits are already class-checked by the FSM; 8 digits max, no
  overflow); the C `int` assignment of the unsigned result is a
  bit-identical u32->i32 wrap.
- `ConvertToChar`'s `buf[buflen] = ANY` can write index 8 of a
  `uint8_t buf[8]` (stack OOB in C, value never read). The Rust
  buffer carries the extra slot so the store is literal, not UB.
- Table event bytes: class-marker rows carry IS_* bit values in an
  `event` u8 exactly as C does; `valid_chars`/`hex_lookup` recomputed
  as provably-equal functions (class masks / digit values) instead of
  copied 256-row literals.

Fidelity quirks kept literal:

- `hex_lookup[c]` returns −1 for non-hex bytes; `iNorm <<= 4;
  iNorm |= −1` poisons every bit. Reachable because class-marker
  rows ALSO match via the raw `m->event == uc` comparison: input
  bytes 0x01/0x02/0x04 are numerically equal to IS_OCT/IS_DEC/IS_HEX
  and therefore match those rows with a garbage byte (CONV-style arms
  then see `hex_lookup[c] = -1`). Kept as C `int` semantics
  (wrapping_shl + sign-extended or); not pinned by the locked
  transcript but transcribed literally.
- The `iNorm == -1` check after a u8 unicode_map read is dead in C
  (cppcheck-suppressed `knownConditionTrueFalse`); kept literally.
- Unescape RET_INV pointer step-back is commented out in C; the
  comment is kept commented. SFCC's step-back (`(*ptr-1) > start`)
  is live and transcribed (usize-guarded, never saturates).
- `PNORM_ACT_WITHIN_QUOTES` SWITCH-falls-through into `default:`
  (no-op) — arm simply ends.
- UNESC_ACT_SPACE returns BEFORE the switch-tail `prev_event = c`
  store when the quote branch fires; the early `return` is literal.
- `multiple_levels` is u8 and `num_spaces` u16 in C; Rust wraps them
  the same way (`wrapping_add`), the alerts compare via `i32::from`.
- C allocates the 65535-byte `decoded_out_vec` on EVERY `JSNorm_exec`
  call whatever the action; Rust allocates the equivalent local Vec
  in the function body (allocation itself is not transcript-visible).
- `js == nullptr` contract: returns RET_QUIT before touching `*ptr`
  or `*bytes_copied` (vector row N12); Rust models it as
  `Option<&mut JsState>` with the same untouched-caller-state shape.

## time/{clock_defs.h,stopwatch.h} (row 17j)

Structural (behavior preserved; default non-TSC config):

- `SnortClock`/`hr_duration`/`hr_time`: libstdc++ `high_resolution_clock`
  == `steady_clock`, `rep = int64_t`, `period = nano`, so durations
  and time points are plain signed 64-bit nanosecond tick counts
  (`i64` aliases). The `USE_TSC_CLOCK` build flavor (`TscClock`, raw
  tick integers, `clock_scale() != 1`) is not this build's config and
  is not ported.
- The C macros (`TO_TICKS`/`TO_USECS`/`TO_NSECS`/`TO_DURATION`/
  `operator""_ticks`/`TIME_POINT_ZERO`/`CLOCK_ZERO`/`DURA_ZERO`)
  become `clock_defs` fns and consts. `TO_DURATION(v, t)` at every
  production site casts into an `hr_duration`, so it is
  micro-to-nano multiplication; a duration-typed first parameter was
  dropped as it can only ever select the same conversion.
- `SnortClock::now()`: C++ steady_clock's epoch is unspecified
  (typically boot); Rust `Instant`'s base is unspecified too. No
  locked vector prints an absolute tick — the port anchors `Instant`
  lazily and reports ns since anchor, reproducing every pinned
  delta/equality observation.
- C++'s `Stopwatch<Clock>` uses the clock only statically (
  `Clock::now()`); Rust carries `PhantomData<C>` (never read).
- `elapsed += get_delta()` / `elapsed + get_delta()` are C signed
  arithmetic on `rep` (wraps on gcc); kept literal as `wrapping_add`
  / `wrapping_sub`.

Quirks pinned, not fixed:

- `DURA_ZERO` literally expands to `Clock::duration::zero()` — scope
  dependent on a name `Clock` existing (inside `Stopwatch` the
  template parameter is that name; upstream's test declares its own
  `struct Clock`). The harness probe therefore pins equivalence via
  the literal `0_ticks` rather than inventing a file-scope `Clock`
  alias upstream never defines.
- Never-started `Stopwatch::start_time`: TSC config seed is explicit
  (`= CLOCK_ZERO`); non-TSC default-initializes, and chrono's
  `time_point` implicit default ctor value-initializes the duration
  to 0 — so the effective value is 0 in BOTH configs. The locked
  harness build is non-TSC (`NONTSC_START_UNSET=0` is not a locked
  vector); both values were confirmed live via a scratch probe
  compiled against the same copy tree with and without
  `-DUSE_TSC_CLOCK` (TSC also revealed `time_point/duration ==
  uint64_t` there — unsigned, unlike the non-TSC signed i64 — one
  more reason the TSC flavor stays a documented, unported
  configuration).
- `cancel()` on a STOPPED clock keeps accumulated lap time (only
  `reset()` clears `elapsed`); upstream's last [stopwatch] SECTION
  pins it and the Rust translation replays it.

## utils/chunk.{h,cc} + lua/{lua.h,lua.cc} (row 17k)

Harness reality, not port fiction:

- Ground truth requires a real LuaJIT: built from source at
  `/opt/data/tools/luajit-src` (git clone, plain `make`; `make Q=1`
  breaks the inner host-CC line as `1gcc` — build without `Q`).
  The C harness links the real static lib, so transcript texts are
  the real parser's.
- `ParseError`/`FatalError` witnesses (stubs.cc) isolate the message
  subsystem; the upstream suite expects nothing from them.

Structural deviations (behavior preserved over the locked corpus):

- `lua_State` modeled as globals map + value Vec; `ManageStack`
  restores via explicit `ms.restore(state)` at each C-exit scope
  (Rust `Drop` cannot borrow a sibling). `State`'s newstate-failure
  `FatalError` branch (process exit) is unrepresentable: kept as a
  doc note.
- Lua source subset parser covers exactly what the locked corpus
  contains: `function NAME() [return <lit>] end`, `NAME = <lit|flat
  table>`, empty chunks, zero/one-arg global calls. Function values
  store only their body's `return` expr (no upvalues/params/locals
  — none of these appear in the corpus).
- LuaJIT `load`/`pcall` split: C's three load sites (chunk, chunk
  exec, args dostring) map to `compile_chunk` + `run_chunk` pairs
  with distinct label rules — loadbuffer chunk label = `name` arg,
  dostring label = the source text (pins `[string "args = {"]:1`
  verbatim in C5's message).
- Message texts for inputs OUTSIDE the locked corpus are generic
  LuaJIT-style strings; only locked strings are pinned. The non-
  function call error "attempt to call a non-function object" is
  shape-only.

Quirks pinned in the Rust flow:

- chunk.cc never pops ParseError's error-message slot; ManageStack's
  `gettop > top` shrink-only restore cleans up at scope exit — and
  C3's number-1 `init` (from C2's `init = 1`) proves globals persist
  across calls on the shared state while isfunction gates the call.

## main/numa.{h,cc} (row 17l)

Harness reality, not port fiction:

- `libnuma`/`hwloc` dev headers are absent on this host; the harness
  witnesses `<numa.h>`/`<numaif.h>`/`<hwloc.h>` (declarations only;
  `NumaWrapper`/`HwlocWrapper` inline bodies never run in the probe).
  The NumaMemPolicy ENUMERATOR VALUES are NOT witness-restated: the
  numaif witness includes the real kernel UAPI `<linux/mempolicy.h>`,
  so the pinned ABI (0/1/2/4) is the kernel's own enum.

Structural deviations (behavior preserved):

- C `enum NumaMemPolicy : uint8_t` + implicit integer casts into the
  enum (the tests stringify raw values) modeled as a Rust `u8`
  newtype-free constant set: every raw value stays representable and
  `stringify`'s default arm runs identically for 3/5/254.
- `NumaWrapper`/`HwlocWrapper` are thin virtual dispatch over libnuma/
  hwloc syscalls: no logic to port; kept as a doc note (the port's
  scope is the pure policy-mapping layer the tests exercise).

## file_api/file_stats.{h,cc} (row 17m)

Harness reality, not port fiction:

- Probe AND gate both `#include` file_stats.cc verbatim (the upstream
  test's own trick) to reach the file-static `file_totals`. The
  upstream test ships its own witness definitions for
  `file_type_name`, `FileCapture` statics, and `FileService`
  statics/coll — the gate thus needs ZERO extra stubs; the probe
  reuses the same witness shapes (buffers_max/in_use constants
  differ: probe witnesses return 41/7, not the test's 0/0).
- `file_service.h` keeps those statics private; the probe's graft
  flips the class to public (same discipline as main/policy.h).
  The gate itself uses only out-of-class definitions, unaffected.
- The ORIGINAL probe ordering exposed a real upstream hazard:
  `file_stats_term()` frees the global WITHOUT nulling; a later
  `file_stats_clear()` memsets through the dangling pointer (exit-
  time SEGV). The locked probe documents this observationally
  (`TERM dangling=1`) and never dereferences freed memory.

Structural deviations (behavior preserved):

- C file-statics/thread-locals -> driver-owned `FileStatsEnv`; the
  freed-global dangling state is modeled as `None` (Rust cannot
  reproduce a dangling deref without UB — the `file_stats_clear`
  post-term memset is skipped in the model).
- `file_stats_sum` adds per-field in declaration order; the flat
  walk is proven equivalent by the all-u64 layout pin (NFLAT=22550
  matches sizeof/8, transcript-pinned).
- `FileStatsHooks` trait = the harness witness seam (file_type_name
  / is_file_signature_enabled / collect_mem_usage /
  collect_cache_usage / buffers_*); C's Builder params on the two
  collect_* witnesses were unused by the bodies the transcripts
  pin, so they are omitted from the seam.
- FileCounts modeled with exact C field order (mechanically diff-
  checked against the header, 31 members) though only the buffer
  pair is read by ported functions.

## file_api/file_identifier.{h,cc} (row 17n)

Harness reality:

- The gate is the TU's own `[FileMagic]` UNIT_TEST block; its six
  cases are tautologies GIVEN production semantics: add_file_id fills
  file_magic_rules[] but NEVER inserts trie nodes, so find_file_type_id
  on any production FileIdentifier is len<=0 -> CONTINUE(0) else
  CONTINUE->UNKNOWN(65535). Both sides reproduce that for the right
  reason (ported traversal + empty trie), not by special-casing.
- The header's private section is grafted public in the harness copy
  (shared by probe AND gate builds): access control only, no semantic
  change; the probe grows a witness trie through the production
  calloc_mem so T-rows pin what the find traversal means once a trie
  exists. Documented, never silent.
- GHash's !static_hash branch references get_random_seed() /
  get_instance_id(); the shim keeps static_hash()==true so the branch
  is dead, but the linker still needs the symbols -> pinned witnesses
  (global get_random_seed per util.h, snort::get_instance_id per
  thread.h) with correct namespaces.

Structural deviations (behavior preserved):

- calloc'd raw node arena (+list of blocks, dtor frees blocks) ->
  Rc<RefCell<IdentifierNode>>; the in/out `void** context` node
  identity survives across find calls, drop covers the dtor's
  snort_free loop.
- memory_usage() tracks the C byte accounting: nodes are costed at
  sizeof(IdentifierNode)=2064 (transcript `sznode=2064`), NOT
  std::mem::size_of::<Rust node>() (Rc/Vec shape is a layout detail,
  pinned as C_IDENTIFIER_NODE_SIZE).
- merge-hash: C stores raw MergeNode{shared,append} blobs in GHash;
  no production path inserts/queries, Rust constructs the GHash with
  the same ctor args (1000, 16, false) as C init_merge_hash.
- get_rule_from_id(id<FILE_ID_MAX) bound + `slot.id > 0` presence
  gate kept literally, including the id-0 quirk (slot 0's id stays 0
  even after add_file_id -> never findable; dup check also never
  fires for id 0).


## file_api/file_lib.{h,cc} (row 17o)

Harness reality:

- Two-mode harness. Gate = file_lib.cc compiled -DUNIT_TEST verbatim:
  its OWN 19 [file_info] cases (334 assertions) run against the real
  TU with ZERO stubs beyond the plain-catch shim; the gate build also
  carries the access-control-only private->public header graft (same
  copy as the probe) so the probe subclass can seed/reset protected
  members. Probe = same TU with the UNIT_TEST block excised plus a
  near-verbatim closure; witnesses ONLY where the upstream .cc files
  drag the DAQ/flow/output monoliths (FileCache, FileService,
  FileFlows, FileInspect ctor, InspectorManager, DetectionEngine,
  PacketTracer, trace, DataBus publish, clock). Every witness body
  prints a WITNESS/FILEDBG/DE/POLICY line, so every transcript line
  that depends on a witness hit cannot fork silently.
- FileCache/FileCache::cached_verdict_lookup/apply_verdict witnesses
  mirror the real bodies' FileInfo-observing parts (file_id==0 gate,
  fc->verdict assignment, pending resume flag return); the hash-table
  bookkeeping itself is what plan row 17p+ will port — the transcript
  pins observable verdict/clock/stat effects only.
- OpenSSL EVP SHA256 is REAL (node headers + libcrypto.so.3 per the
  NEXT.md recipe): D1 'abc', G4/G6C digests and sha_to_string UPPERCASE
  rendering all pin production byte values.
- Deterministic clock: filib_enable_fake_clock interposes the
  clock_gettime used by ts_() so 'duration-0.000000'/'-0.250000' lines
  are exact; file_stats_init() (real body) arms the THREAD_LOCAL
  file_stats a null deref would otherwise hit.

Structural deviations (behavior preserved):

- C std::string FileInfo members (file_name/url/host_name/
  extracted_file_name) hold arbitrary bytes in the transcript (0xFF
  BOM, raw UTF-16LE) -> Vec<u8> fields, not String. All getters hand
  out &[u8]; the UTF-8 print path (helpers/utf get_UTF8_fname) stays
  behind the FileContext seam until its row.
- Pinned UB now panics loudly: the deserialize header memcpy and the
  serialize string memcpy are unguarded in C (reads past the declared
  buffer); the Rust port indexes slices equivalently, so a truncated
  buffer panics instead of reading OOB. The A..E4 replay never hits
  those paths (the probe crafts buffers that stay in bounds); any
  future fuzz row must treat them as panic-by-construction.
- sha256: Option<[u8; 32]> replaces the new[]/delete[] member; C's
  copy-conditional ("src null sha never clears dst") and
  reset_sha's conditional sig_state flip are transcription-literal.
- FileInfo::operator= / copy are explicit assign/copy(&other) pair
  (self-assign guard via ptr::eq); trait-object fields
  (user_file_data/file_capture seams) rule out a derive.
- FileContext: NOT ported this row (cache/service/flows/capture
  closure). The harness G-blocks pin its observable behavior for the
  next rows; Rust keeps no FileContext type yet — deliberately, so a
  half-seam cannot drift.
- VerdictName is [&str; 8] including the FILE_VERDICT_MAX ->
  "INVALID" slot (A_VNAMES transcript pins index 7).
- FileCounts reserve-failure accounting goes through the
  driver-owned FileCounts value (same discipline as row 17m) rather
  than a thread-local static.


## file_api/file_cache.{h,cc} (row 17p)

Harness reality:

- Two-mode harness `tools/fcache_harness`. Gate = file_cache.cc compiled
  -DUNIT_TEST VERBATIM: its OWN 7 [file_cache] cases (42 assertions,
  runner-measured) pass with the plain-catch shim. Probe links the same
  TU with the UNIT_TEST block inert, plus now-REAL hash/xhash.cc,
  hash/hash_lru_cache.cc and time/packet_time.cc TUs. The donor
  `FileCache::*` witness block from tools/filib_harness/stubs.cc is
  RETIRED at this link (real TU defines the symbols); filib's committed
  transcript is NOT touched -- the vanished witness lines in the new
  transcript are the retirement signal (NEXT.md rule).
- FileHashKey wire layout probe-verified from the real headers with
  g++ on the harness grafts: sizeof=56, sip@0 sgroup@18 dip@20
  dgroup@38 file_id@40 asid@48 padding@50; sfip::SfIp=18 (ip8[16] +
  family i16); sizeof(FileCache::FileNode)=24; sizeof(timeval)=16.
- Determinism: the FILE-CACHE clock is packet time, not SnortClock.
  Real packet_time.cc's THREAD_LOCAL s_recent_packet is armed ONLY
  through packet_time_update (it clamps backwards calls); the CLK
  tv=... vectors steps pin every expiry math. The SnortClock
  clock_gettime interposition carried from the donor is orthogonal.
  THREAD_LOCAL file_counts armed via real file_stats_init() (rc 139
  otherwise, NEXT.md).

Structural deviations (behavior preserved):

- `std::mutex cache_mutex`, `std::recursive_mutex
  user_file_data_mutex` and the `std::atomic<int64_t>`
  block/lookup timeouts are NOT reproduced: the port models the
  single-threaded data plane the gates run with, Rust exclusive
  borrows standing in for the locks (precedent
  hash/lru_cache_shared.rs). `CacheNodePin` keeps the C pin/unpin
  accounting faithfully; M_PINNED_SKIP pins the pinned-entry expiry
  skip and clear()'s unconditional force-free.
- XHash's `void* data` slot carries an index into the wrapper's
  FileNode arena (no pointer casts). C stores FileNode BY VALUE in
  the hash node's datasize block (`(FileNode*)hnode->data`); the
  port keeps it in the arena and clones the one Rc handle. The
  C++ `ExpectedFileCache::is_node_recovery_ok` / `free_user_data`
  virtuals are re-asserted by the wrapper around XHash's pin-list
  hook (hash/xhash.rs precedent): not-recoverable node keys pinned
  before insert; any ANR victim detected after the insert by its
  dead back-link, dropping the FileContext exactly as
  `free_user_data` deletes it (file_cache.cc:68-91).
- C clear() force-frees EVEN pinned entries (delete under a
  paused-threads precondition, file_cache.h:85-87 -- dangling by
  design upstream). Rust drops the Rc instead: no dangling deref is
  constructible, and the M block's freed=1/remaining=0 semantics
  replay exactly.
- THREAD_LOCAL `file_counts` is a driver-owned `&mut FileCounts` at
  every counting call site (rows 17m/17o discipline).
  `ErrorMessage(...)` inside set_max_files is a comment until the
  log row; the clamp itself (instance_max+1) is live and witnessed.
- C overloads `get_file` at 4 and 7 params; Rust cannot: `get_file`
  (private, 7-arg) + `get_file_public` (4-arg).
- The `FileInfo*`/`FileInfo&` parameters of
  publish_flow_verdict_locked / store_shared_verdict /
  take_pending_file_data are FileContext handles in every real path;
  the port threads the whole handle and compares aliasing with
  `Rc::ptr_eq` instead of raw pointer `==` (mirrors the file_lib
  assign/copy ptr::eq self-guards, row 17o; each aliased C++ case is
  a self-guarded no-op).
- `make_key` keeps C's uint32 `addressSpaceId` -> uint16 `asid`
  truncation (file_cache.cc:371, file_cache.h:47) and the
  deliberate client/server swap (dip=client_ip, sip=server_ip) --
  pinned by F_ASID_TRUNC/F_ASID_SAME, not "fixed".
- `new FileContext` reaches the globals
  (InspectorManager::acquire_file_inspector(),
  SnortConfig::get_conf()); `FileCache::new_file_context` gates nulls
  exactly as the C++ ctor (file_lib.cc:559-569). `FileContext`
  itself stays the row-17o seam: only the members file_cache.cc
  touches are carried; the full body lands with FileFlows/FileContext
  (row 17q+).
- FILE_DEBUG/file_trace trace lines omitted exactly as in file_lib.rs
  (row 17o precedent); the C call sites are kept as comments. The
  harness FILEDBG lines pin the format strings C-side.
- Control-console dump path: `printf("%-20s")` byte-width semantics
  via `pad_c`; byte-carrying names render through
  `String::from_utf8_lossy` (names carry raw 0xFF/UTF-16, 17o).
  dump_cache matches sha hex CASE-SENSITIVELY (lowercase probe string
  misses, uppercase hits; >64 chars miss) -- pinned, not normalized.
- `log_file_event`'s DataBus body is row 17q (file_lib.cc:613); the
  call sites stay literal through `FileCacheEventSink`.

## file_api/file_flows.{h,cc} (row 17q)

Harness reality:

- Two-mode harness `tools/fflows_harness` (fcache donor recipe lifted).
  Gate = file_flows.cc compiled -DUNIT_TEST VERBATIM: its OWN 8
  [file_flows][lifecycle] cases (22 assertions, runner-measured) pass
  with the plain-catch shim — the block ships its own `FFTestAccessor`
  friend struct + `FF_TEST_CONTEXT : public FileContext`, so NO
  private->public graft is needed on the gate side. Probe = the same TU
  with the block inert, linked against the REAL file_cache.cc +
  file_lib.cc + file_stats + capture closure.
- The donor fcache `FileFlows::*` witness block is RETIRED at this link
  only (real TU defines the symbols); fcache's committed transcript is
  NOT touched (vanishing witness lines = retirement signal, NEXT.md
  rule). `init_file_context` is a DEAD declaration upstream (grep-
  proven: no definition, no call site in snort3-src) — retiring its
  witness costs nothing; the port records it as a comment, not code.
- The ONLY graft delta over the fcache closure donor: `flowz/flow_fwd.h`
  Flow extended with `get_flow_data`/`set_flow_data` (flow.h:195/200),
  the single-slot FlowData store the harness pins; `FIXACC` extended to
  file_api.h (access-only). New witnesses: NetworkPolicy
  get_base_file_policy pinned storage, `snort::get_file_processed_size`
  (file_service.cc:256, free linkage), PacketTracer get_time/
  restart_timer + armable is_daq_activated/get_instance_id/
  get_max_file_depth/is_file_service_enabled, DetectionEngine
  queue_event line, FileService get_max_file_depth passthrough.

Structural deviations (behavior preserved):

- C raw owning `FileContext*` slots (current_context, main_context,
  deferred_delete_context, ppc map) -> `Rc<RefCell<FileContext>>`. The
  C use-after-free windows the upstream gate cases pin ("caller deref
  after remove is safe", the retire-then-set orderings) are
  STRUCTURALLY EXCLUDED by Rc, but every null/erase/park ORDER is
  transcribed literally, so the observable slot state matches every
  transcript line (the C `deferred == a` / `current == null` CHECKs
  have exact Rust analogues via `Rc::ptr_eq`).
- The m3_new=%d probe vector compares the fresh main_context against
  the DANGLING old-main pointer; glibc hands `new FileContext` the
  just-freed chunk so C reads them EQUAL (m3==m2 pinned 0 across every
  rebuild). The pinned vector value is verbatim; the dangling compare
  itself cannot and need not be constructed in Rust (UAF structurally
  excluded above).
- `std::mutex file_flow_context_mutex` (file_flows.h:61) is declared
  but NEVER locked in this TU; the port models Rust exclusivity in
  place of the field (rows 17m/17p deviation pattern).
- `Flow* flow` lives in the Flow's FlowData slot in C; FileFlows
  carries the fcache-cache-flow view and the C file-scope statics
  `FileFlows::file_flow_data_id` (file_flows.cc:56), `static
  THREAD_LOCAL uint32_t max_file_id` (:57) and the factory's FlowData
  slot ride `FileEnvLite` Cells plus a driver-passed slot handle at
  get_file_flows — the CacheFlow Copy set from 17p stays untouched;
  flow->service lookups ride the FileEnvLite cell.
- The two `file_process` overloads split into `file_process_multi`
  (file_id form, file_flows.cc:388) and `file_process` (single-file
  form, :499) — Rust has no overloading.
- `FilePolicyBase*` -> `FilePolicyHandle` (file_cache.rs): the C
  add_ref / `FilePolicyBase::delete_file_policy` pair (file_api.cc:
  28-41, file_flows.h:120-126) runs through the handle at the exact C
  points (refs=1->2 at B_CREATE, policy_refs=2->1 at C5_DTOR); the C
  `delete policy` at count 0 = the Box drop inside delete_file_policy.
  Handles in the same store compare via `Rc::ptr_eq`.
- `std::unordered_map<uint64_t, FileContext*>
  partially_processed_contexts` -> insertion-order Vec of (id, ctx):
  every C observation here is a point find / size() / count / the dtor
  retire walk, and the pinned multi-entry walks retire equal-line
  contexts, so hash order is unobservable (fcache OtnMap precedent).
- `EventGen` REG_TEST-only members (events_generated, none_found,
  get_raw, bitmask) compile out in the harness build config and are
  not ported; `std::bitset<EVENT_MAX>` -> lazily sized Vec<bool>
  (helpers/event_gen.rs; the bitset order is never observed).
- FileConfig access is TWO views (FileConfigView read surface for the
  flows ladder, FileConfigLite construction) because the C TU reads
  `conf` globals through different owners than the 17p cache seam —
  source-compat with the committed 17p witnesses preserved via defaulted
  PacketTracerApi methods (is_daq_activated/restart_timer/daq_log).
- The heavy FileContext half stays pinned-panic seams with C-line
  cites (process pair :825/:986, find_file_type_from_ips :1004 — the
